Preface
Why This Book Exists
Bodybuilding has always been about more than muscle. It’s about the pursuit of mastery —
mastery over training, nutrition, recovery, and ultimately, the body itself. But when enhancement
enters the picture, the game changes. The stakes rise. The line between progress and damage
becomes razor thin.
Anabolic steroids, growth hormone, insulin, peptides, and novel agonists can reshape a physique
in ways no natural athlete can touch. But they also place enormous stress on the cardiovascular
system, liver, kidneys, endocrine axis, and even mental health. Many athletes ignore this reality
until it’s too late — chasing size, strength, or stage conditioning while their blood tells a very
different story.
This book exists to close that gap. It is a field manual for enhanced bodybuilders — not just a
catalog of drugs or diet tricks, but a guide to precision. It combines bloodwork interpretation,
physiology, training, nutrition, and psychology into one integrated system. Because building a
world-class physique isn’t just about “more gear, more food, more weight.” It’s about knowing
exactly what’s happening inside your body at every stage of the transformation, and steering it
with intelligence, not blind risk.
This book is built for the lifter who refuses to guess. For the coach who takes ownership of their
athletes’ health. For the clinician who wants a framework to understand the enhanced athlete’s
reality. And most of all, it’s for the serious bodybuilder who believes discipline is more than
training hard — it’s the relentless pursuit of precision.

Who This Book Is For
•
•
•
•

The Athlete — Competitive or not, who uses performance-enhancing drugs (PEDs) and
wants to maximize progress without sabotaging their health.
The Coach — Who guides enhanced lifters and wants to merge performance with
longevity instead of trading one for the other.
The Clinician — Who works with bodybuilders and needs to interpret labs in the context
of enhancement, not just “general population medicine.”
The Serious Lifter — Who treats bodybuilding as a craft, even if the stage isn’t the goal,
and refuses to drift blindly through cycles.

What This Book Is Not
•
•
•

Not medical advice. You’ll find education, strategies, and context — not prescriptions or
guarantees.
Not harm-free fantasy. PEDs and extreme diets carry risk. Labs help manage risk, not
erase it.
Not fear-mongering. We don’t preach abstinence, nor glorify recklessness. This book is
about balance — the balance of performance and health, short-term results and long-term
sustainability.

Introduction
The Case for Bloodwork in Bodybuilding
The difference between a successful career in bodybuilding and a short-lived disaster often
comes down to one thing: feedback. You can feel strong in the gym while your arteries are
clogging. You can look lean while your thyroid is shutting down. You can add 20 pounds of
muscle while silently building liver failure.
Bloodwork is not a bureaucratic medical task — it’s a performance tool. It is the data set that
reveals whether your strategies are working or slowly killing you. Every decision — which
compound to run, when to push food, when to diet harder, when to back off — can be sharpened
by labs. In the enhanced bodybuilding era, ignoring bloodwork is not just careless, it’s a betrayal
of your own potential.

Performance vs Longevity: The Balancing Act
Every enhanced athlete walks a tightrope. On one side lies performance — the size, strength,
and conditioning needed to dominate on stage or in life. On the other side lies longevity —
cardiovascular health, hormonal stability, mental well-being, and years of training left in the
tank.
The question isn’t “performance or longevity?” It’s “how much of each are you willing to trade,
and when?” That’s where precision comes in. Bloodwork tells you where the line is. It tells you
when you can push harder, when you need to pivot, and when you’ve crossed into danger.
Without it, you’re guessing — and bodybuilding has no mercy for guesswork.

Transformation as a System: Bulk, Recomp, Cut, Prep
Physique transformation is not one-dimensional. You don’t simply bulk until you’re fat, cut until
you’re stringy, and pray it all works out. Precision means building an integrated system where
every phase is mapped, measured, and executed with intention:
•

lean bulk — Labs tell you when your lipids are straining, when hematocrit is creeping
too high, or when insulin sensitivity is slipping. Training focuses on progression without
injury. Nutrition builds mass without fat spillover. Psychology keeps you disciplined
when appetite fades.

•

•

•

Recomp — Labs monitor hormones, thyroid, and glucose as you chase the delicate dance
of fat loss with muscle gain. PEDs are tuned for balance. Training sustains strength.
Nutrition cycles calories with intent. Psychology keeps you steady when progress is
subtle.
cutting phase — Labs track thyroid output, cortisol, and blood pressure in the grind of
deficit. PEDs preserve muscle and harden the look. Training intensity is balanced against
recovery. Nutrition dials in macros, refeeds, and sodium balance. Psychology keeps you
focused when hunger and fatigue tempt you to quit.
Contest Prep — The crucible. Labs watch kidney, liver, and electrolyte status as stress
hits max. PEDs peak performance while risking greater strain. Training and diet narrow
to surgical precision. Psychology becomes everything: tunnel vision, resilience, and the
will to endure.

Each phase is not just “diet and train.” It’s diet, train, dose, monitor, adjust, repeat — a closed
loop of input, feedback, and adaptation. That loop is what separates champions from casualties.

The Ethos of Discipline and Ownership
You can’t build a great physique without discipline. But discipline isn’t just training through
pain or sticking to a diet. True discipline is ownership.
Ownership means you don’t wait for a doctor or a coach to tell you what’s happening — you
track it yourself. Ownership means pulling your labs, reading them, and making the changes
required, even if it means swallowing pride. Ownership means not blaming the cycle, the prep,
or genetics when things go wrong. It means acknowledging: I chose this path. I own the
outcomes.
And that’s the central philosophy of this book: discipline is freedom. The freedom to pursue
extreme performance without blind risk. The freedom to keep transforming your physique year
after year instead of burning out in one season. The freedom to chase greatness with precision
instead of chance.

What Lies Ahead
The chapters that follow are divided into five major sections:
•
•
•

Foundations — Understanding bloodwork basics, endocrine physiology, and how to
read labs like an athlete.
Anabolic Families — How each drug class affects markers, from testosterone esters to
designer orals.
Hormones & Agonists — Growth hormone, insulin, GLPs, peptides, and their synergy
with labs.

•
•

Systems at Risk — Cardiovascular, liver, kidney, hematology, and even mental health.
Physique Transformation Strategies — lean bulk, recomp, cut, and contest prep, with
bloodwork, PEDs, training, diet, and psychology integrated.

This is not a casual read. It’s a manual — meant to be referenced, studied, applied, and lived.
Every field chart, every case study, every lab interpretation is designed to help you make better
decisions in real time.
The enhanced path is not safe. It’s not simple. But with precision, ownership, and discipline, it
can be sustainable — and it can lead to the kind of physique transformation that defines a
lifetime.

Chapter 1 — The Fundamentals of
Bloodwork
Bloodwork is the foundation of precision bodybuilding. Without it, you’re guessing. With it,
you’re driving your transformation with the same clarity a coach uses when tracking progressive
overload or macros. This chapter will give you the blueprint: which labs to order, when to test,
how to read them, and what red flags demand immediate action.

Why Labs Matter More for the enhanced athlete
Every athlete has a tolerance ceiling. Enhanced bodybuilding lowers that ceiling faster.
Anabolics elevate red blood cell count, skew lipids, strain the liver, and alter hormone balance.
Growth hormone shifts glucose metabolism and can thicken soft tissue. insulin, if mismanaged,
creates chaos.
All of these effects show up in labs before they become symptoms. That’s why serious lifters and
competitive athletes treat bloodwork not as an “annual checkup” but as a performance and
health monitoring system.
Labs tell you when:
•
•
•
•
•

Your hematocrit is climbing into clot territory.
Your liver enzymes are beyond a training-related spike.
Your HDL has bottomed out while LDL is climbing.
Your kidneys are under silent strain.
Your thyroid and sex hormones are adapting (or crashing).

Ignore those signs, and you won’t just lose performance — you may lose your health
permanently.

What Labs to Order (Basic → Extended)
Think of bloodwork like training progression. Start with a solid base, then layer on complexity as
needed. Here’s how to structure it:

Baseline Panels (Every enhanced athlete, Every Time)
•

CBC (complete blood count): Hematocrit, hemoglobin, red/white cells, platelets.

•
•
•
•
•

CMP (Comprehensive metabolic panel): Liver (AST, ALT, ALP, bilirubin), kidney
(creatinine, eGFR, BUN), electrolytes.
Lipid Panel: HDL, LDL, triglycerides, total cholesterol.
Hormones: Total testosterone, free testosterone, estradiol (E2, sensitive assay), SHBG,
LH, FSH, prolactin.
Thyroid: TSH, Free T3, Free T4.
Glucose Control: Fasting glucose, HbA1c, insulin.

Extended Panels (Recommended for Enhanced Athletes)
•
•
•
•
•

Cardiovascular Risk: ApoB, LDL-P, lipoprotein(a), homocysteine, CRP (hs-CRP).
Kidney Precision: Cystatin-C, urinalysis (protein, microalbumin).
Endocrine Detail: DHT, progesterone, cortisol, IGF-1.
Hematology Extension: Ferritin, iron, EPO.
Specialized Markers: PSA (if on DHTs), GGT (alcohol vs oral steroid liver stress),
troponin (if cardiac strain suspected).

When to Test
Timing is everything. Just like you periodize training, you must periodize labs around your PED
use and physique phases.
•
•
•
•
•

Baseline (before any cycle): Your true natural reference point. Absolutely nonnegotiable.
Mid-Cycle (6–8 weeks in): See how the body is adapting under drug load. Adjust if
needed.
Pre-Contest (2–4 weeks out): Spot dangerous red flags before peak week.
Post-Cycle (4–6 weeks after): Assess recovery, hormone status, organ function.
Off-Season (every 3–4 months): Even when “cruising,” you’re not immune to risk.

Rule of thumb: more compounds = more frequent labs. Heavy oral use, high doses, or
stacking GH/insulin means you pull more often.

How to Interpret Lab Slips
Doctors often read labs as “normal” or “abnormal.” That’s not enough for athletes. Bodybuilders
need to interpret labs in context:
•

Normal but Sub-Optimal: A lab might be within population range but still poor for
performance (e.g., HDL of 40 is “normal” but inadequate for a PED user).

•

•

False Alarms from Training: Heavy training can elevate AST/ALT (liver enzymes) or
CK (muscle breakdown). Learn to distinguish training-related spikes from true liver
damage (GGT helps here).
Individual Baselines: Some lifters run naturally high hematocrit or low SHBG. Know
your normal before enhancement.

Think of it like adjusting macros: population “normal” is the RDA; athlete “normal” is what
optimizes your performance and keeps you in the game.

Red Flags Demanding Immediate Action
Certain lab results are not negotiable — they demand a pullback, a medical consult, or an
intervention:
•
•
•
•
•
•

Hematocrit > 55% — Blood viscosity risk; stroke/clot danger.
AST/ALT > 3x upper limit + GGT elevation — True liver stress, not just training.
eGFR < 60 or protein in urine — Kidney function decline; serious risk.
HDL < 25 with LDL > 160 — Cardiovascular danger zone.
Fasting glucose consistently > 110 mg/dL or A1c > 5.8% — insulin resistance.
Prolactin > 25 ng/mL with symptoms — Sexual dysfunction, gyno, mood issues.

Red flags are not “wait and see.” They are stop signs. Enhanced athletes must treat them with the
same urgency as an injury — push through them, and the damage becomes permanent.

The Takeaway
Bloodwork isn’t about fear — it’s about freedom. Freedom to push your body to its limits
without losing sight of your health. Freedom to bulk, cut, recomp, and prep with precision
instead of guesswork. Freedom to extend your career instead of ending it early.
If you train with discipline and eat with discipline, why wouldn’t you test with discipline?
Precision bloodwork is the third pillar of enhanced bodybuilding, standing alongside training and
nutrition. Master it, and you master the game.

Chapter 2 — The Endocrine Foundation
Hormones are the command system of the body. They regulate energy, recovery, mood, libido,
muscle growth, and fat storage. In natural physiology, the endocrine system is a self-regulating
symphony. In enhanced bodybuilding, it becomes a chemistry experiment — one that can deliver
world-class physiques or total collapse, depending on precision.
This chapter covers the big five hormones that define the enhanced athlete’s internal landscape:
testosterone, estrogen, DHT, prolactin, and SHBG.

Testosterone — The Master Signal
Natural Role
•
•
•

Produced primarily in the testes under LH (luteinizing hormone) stimulation.
Drives muscle protein synthesis, libido, mood stability, and red blood cell production.
Balanced within a feedback loop: more test = less LH/FSH via negative feedback.

Enhanced Context
Exogenous testosterone bypasses natural production, creating supraphysiological levels. The
body shuts down LH and FSH (testes go offline), but serum testosterone rises far above natural
ranges.
Lab Signatures:
•
•
•

Total testosterone: baseline 300–1,000 ng/dL → enhanced 2,000–7,000+ ng/dL.
Free testosterone: the real driver of anabolic activity, often disproportionately elevated if
SHBG is suppressed.
Hematocrit rises (EPO stimulation), requiring careful monitoring.

Estrogen — Friend, Not Foe
Natural Role
•
•
•

Aromatization of testosterone → estradiol (E2).
Regulates joint health, libido, mood, and cardiovascular protection.
Critical for muscle growth synergy (testosterone without estrogen is less anabolic).

Enhanced Context

High test = high aromatization = elevated E2. Too high: water retention, gyno risk, mood swings,
blood pressure spikes. Too low (from aggressive AI use): joint pain, depression, poor libido,
impaired muscle gain.
Lab Signatures:
•
•

Estradiol (sensitive assay): natural optimal ~20–40 pg/mL. Enhanced: often 50–70 pg/mL
without issues, but individual response varies.
The key is not just the number — but the symptom profile (is high E2 manageable or
destructive?).

Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) — The Hardener
Natural Role
•
•
•

A potent androgen formed when 5α-reductase converts testosterone → DHT.
Strongly binds androgen receptors: influences strength, hardness, libido, aggression.
Critical for male sexual development.

Enhanced Context
•
•
•

Elevated with DHT-derived compounds (Masteron, Proviron, Anavar, Winstrol).
Excess → scalp hair loss, prostate hypertrophy, skin oiliness.
Beneficial → increased strength, hardness, and mood stabilization in some.

Lab Signatures:
•
•

DHT range: 30–85 ng/dL baseline; enhanced athletes often far above.
PSA (prostate-specific antigen) becomes a relevant marker, especially >35 years old.

Prolactin — The Silent Saboteur
Natural Role
•
•

Produced in the pituitary; regulates reproductive function and lactation in women.
In men, it’s normally low, with little anabolic role.

Enhanced Context
•
•

Elevated by 19-nors (nandrolone, trenbolone, trestolone) via progestin pathways.
High prolactin → sexual dysfunction, low libido, erectile dysfunction, mood instability.

•

When combined with high estrogen, prolactin amplifies gyno risk.

Lab Signatures:
•
•

Normal: 2–18 ng/mL.
25 ng/mL with symptoms = intervention needed.

Sex Hormone Binding Globulin (SHBG) — The Regulator
Natural Role
•
•
•

A binding protein that regulates how much testosterone/estrogen are bioavailable.
High SHBG → less free hormone; Low SHBG → more free hormone.
Influenced by thyroid, insulin, diet, and liver health.

Enhanced Context
•
•
•

Androgens suppress SHBG.
Low SHBG → high free test (good for growth), but also more unstable hormone swings.
Extremely low SHBG (<10 nmol/L) → poor hormone balance, increased side effects.

Lab Signatures:
•
•
•

Natural range: 20–60 nmol/L.
Enhanced athletes often see <15 nmol/L.
Low SHBG also correlates with insulin resistance, tying into metabolic health.

How Bodybuilding Alters the Endocrine Web
In natural physiology, testosterone, estrogen, SHBG, and prolactin exist in balance.
Enhancement disrupts every axis:
•
•
•
•

High testosterone suppresses LH/FSH → shutdown of natural testicular production.
High aromatization elevates E2 → requires AI/SERM management.
Exogenous DHTs lower SHBG → amplifying free hormone swings.
19-Nors elevate prolactin/progesterone → destabilizing sexual and mood balance.

The athlete’s challenge is not to eliminate these hormones, but to balance them intelligently for
performance and health.

The endocrine system is not just “testosterone and estrogen.” It’s an interactive web where each
hormone pushes and pulls on the others. Enhanced athletes cannot afford to treat hormones as
isolated — every change ripples through the system.
Mastering the endocrine foundation means more than chasing high testosterone. It means
optimizing estrogen, controlling prolactin, balancing SHBG, and understanding how DHTderived compounds reshape the landscape. Get this right, and you’ll not only build more muscle,
you’ll stay in the game longer.

Chapter 2 — The Endocrine Foundation
Hormones are the command system of the body. They regulate energy, recovery, mood, libido,
muscle growth, and fat storage. In natural physiology, the endocrine system is a self-regulating
symphony. In enhanced bodybuilding, it becomes a chemistry experiment — one that can deliver
world-class physiques or total collapse, depending on precision.
This chapter covers the big five hormones that define the enhanced athlete’s internal landscape:
testosterone, estrogen, DHT, prolactin, and SHBG.

Testosterone — The Master Signal
Natural Role
•
•
•

Produced primarily in the testes under LH (luteinizing hormone) stimulation.
Drives muscle protein synthesis, libido, mood stability, and red blood cell production.
Balanced within a feedback loop: more test = less LH/FSH via negative feedback.

Enhanced Context
Exogenous testosterone bypasses natural production, creating supraphysiological levels. The
body shuts down LH and FSH (testes go offline), but serum testosterone rises far above natural
ranges.
Lab Signatures:
•
•
•

Total testosterone: baseline 300–1,000 ng/dL → enhanced 2,000–7,000+ ng/dL.
Free testosterone: the real driver of anabolic activity, often disproportionately elevated if
SHBG is suppressed.
Hematocrit rises (EPO stimulation), requiring careful monitoring.

Estrogen — Friend, Not Foe
Natural Role
•
•
•

Aromatization of testosterone → estradiol (E2).
Regulates joint health, libido, mood, and cardiovascular protection.
Critical for muscle growth synergy (testosterone without estrogen is less anabolic).

Enhanced Context

High test = high aromatization = elevated E2. Too high: water retention, gyno risk, mood swings,
blood pressure spikes. Too low (from aggressive AI use): joint pain, depression, poor libido,
impaired muscle gain.
Lab Signatures:
•
•

Estradiol (sensitive assay): natural optimal ~20–40 pg/mL. Enhanced: often 50–70 pg/mL
without issues, but individual response varies.
The key is not just the number — but the symptom profile (is high E2 manageable or
destructive?).

Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) — The Hardener
Natural Role
•
•
•

A potent androgen formed when 5α-reductase converts testosterone → DHT.
Strongly binds androgen receptors: influences strength, hardness, libido, aggression.
Critical for male sexual development.

Enhanced Context
•
•
•

Elevated with DHT-derived compounds (Masteron, Proviron, Anavar, Winstrol).
Excess → scalp hair loss, prostate hypertrophy, skin oiliness.
Beneficial → increased strength, hardness, and mood stabilization in some.

Lab Signatures:
•
•

DHT range: 30–85 ng/dL baseline; enhanced athletes often far above.
PSA (prostate-specific antigen) becomes a relevant marker, especially >35 years old.

Prolactin — The Silent Saboteur
Natural Role
•
•

Produced in the pituitary; regulates reproductive function and lactation in women.
In men, it’s normally low, with little anabolic role.

Enhanced Context
•
•

Elevated by 19-nors (nandrolone, trenbolone, trestolone) via progestin pathways.
High prolactin → sexual dysfunction, low libido, erectile dysfunction, mood instability.

•

When combined with high estrogen, prolactin amplifies gyno risk.

Lab Signatures:
•
•

Normal: 2–18 ng/mL.
25 ng/mL with symptoms = intervention needed.

Sex Hormone Binding Globulin (SHBG) — The Regulator
Natural Role
•
•
•

A binding protein that regulates how much testosterone/estrogen are bioavailable.
High SHBG → less free hormone; Low SHBG → more free hormone.
Influenced by thyroid, insulin, diet, and liver health.

Enhanced Context
•
•
•

Androgens suppress SHBG.
Low SHBG → high free test (good for growth), but also more unstable hormone swings.
Extremely low SHBG (<10 nmol/L) → poor hormone balance, increased side effects.

Lab Signatures:
•
•
•

Natural range: 20–60 nmol/L.
Enhanced athletes often see <15 nmol/L.
Low SHBG also correlates with insulin resistance, tying into metabolic health.

How Bodybuilding Alters the Endocrine Web
In natural physiology, testosterone, estrogen, SHBG, and prolactin exist in balance.
Enhancement disrupts every axis:
•
•
•
•

High testosterone suppresses LH/FSH → shutdown of natural testicular production.
High aromatization elevates E2 → requires AI/SERM management.
Exogenous DHTs lower SHBG → amplifying free hormone swings.
19-Nors elevate prolactin/progesterone → destabilizing sexual and mood balance.

The athlete’s challenge is not to eliminate these hormones, but to balance them intelligently for
performance and health.

The Takeaway
The endocrine system is not just “testosterone and estrogen.” It’s an interactive web where each
hormone pushes and pulls on the others. Enhanced athletes cannot afford to treat hormones as
isolated — every change ripples through the system.
Mastering the endocrine foundation means more than chasing high testosterone. It means
optimizing estrogen, controlling prolactin, balancing SHBG, and understanding how DHTderived compounds reshape the landscape. Get this right, and you’ll not only build more muscle,
you’ll stay in the game longer.

Chapter 4 — Testosterone & Its Esters
If bloodwork is the dashboard of enhanced bodybuilding, testosterone is the engine. It is the
foundation compound — the hormone every cycle, every stack, and every protocol is built on.
Whether you’re running a lean bulk, a contest prep, or a simple cruise, testosterone defines the
landscape of your labs and your physique.
This chapter explores the physiology of testosterone, the differences between esters, how they
show up on labs, and what athletes must monitor to stay in the game.

Physiological Baseline: Testosterone in the Human Body
•
•
•
•

Produced mainly in the testes, under control of LH and FSH.
Drives muscle protein synthesis, strength, libido, mood stability, bone density, and
RBC production.
Natural levels fluctuate daily and seasonally, ranging from ~300–1,000 ng/dL in men.
Balanced by aromatization (→ estrogen), 5α-reduction (→ DHT), and binding proteins
(SHBG).

When exogenous testosterone is introduced, the natural loop is bypassed: LH/FSH drop,
endogenous production shuts down, and circulating testosterone skyrockets.

Testosterone Esters Explained
Testosterone itself is identical across all preparations — what changes is the ester, which
modifies absorption rate and half-life.
•
•
•
•
•

Cypionate — Half-life ~7–8 days. U.S. standard for TRT. Stable, predictable.
Enanthate — Half-life ~5–7 days. Nearly interchangeable with cypionate.
Propionate — Half-life ~2–3 days. Requires frequent injections but offers tighter control
and faster clearance.
Suspension (no ester) — Pure testosterone. Extremely fast-acting, often painful, rarely
used outside advanced contest prep.
Undecanoate — Longest ester. Half-life ~20–30 days. Rare in bodybuilding due to poor
control of levels.

Field insight: For most athletes, the choice between cypionate and enanthate is preference or
availability. Propionate is often reserved for prep when water control matters. Suspension and
undecanoate are niche tools.

Lab Signatures of Testosterone Use
Testosterone leaves a clear fingerprint on bloodwork. Key markers:
•
•
•
•
•
•
•

Total Testosterone → Elevated massively (2,000–7,000+ ng/dL on cycle).
Free Testosterone → Elevated even more if SHBG is suppressed.
Estradiol (E2) → Increased via aromatization; balance depends on AI/SERM use.
SHBG → Suppressed, often <15 nmol/L.
Hematocrit/Hemoglobin → Elevated via increased erythropoiesis; risk of polycythemia.
Lipids → HDL decreases, LDL increases (dose-dependent).
Liver Enzymes → Usually stable unless oral kickstarts are included.

Risks & Considerations
•
•
•
•

Cardiovascular — Low HDL, high LDL, increased hematocrit = higher clot and heart
strain risk.
Estrogenic — Gyno, water retention, blood pressure spikes from high aromatization.
Androgenic — Acne, hair loss, prostate stimulation (especially if DHT rises).
Endocrine Shutdown — Complete suppression of natural production. PCT or permanent
TRT becomes inevitable after long-term use.

Interpreting Labs in Practice
•
•
•
•
•

Hematocrit >52% → Consider blood donation or lowering dose.
HDL <35 mg/dL → Add lipid support (omega-3s, citrus bergamot, statins if necessary).
E2 >80 pg/mL with symptoms → AI/SERM or dose adjustment.
SHBG <10 nmol/L → Expect more hormonal volatility; labs should be pulled more
frequently.
PSA rising → Monitor prostate health, especially in athletes 35+.

The Takeaway
Testosterone is the backbone of enhancement. It sets the hormonal stage, dictates lab shifts, and
drives both progress and risk. The ester doesn’t change the hormone — it only changes how fast
it acts and how stable your levels stay.
For the enhanced athlete, managing testosterone isn’t just about “more equals better.” It’s about
balancing estrogen, controlling hematocrit, monitoring lipids, and understanding how each ester
plays into the training and diet phase you’re in.
Master testosterone, and you master the foundation. Fail to respect it, and every other compound
you add will only amplify chaos.

Chapter 5 — DHT-Derived Compounds: The
Sculptors of Physique
Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) derivatives are bodybuilding’s chisels. Where testosterone lays down
the clay, DHT-based anabolics carve it into sharpness, density, and definition. They don’t
aromatize into estrogen, they hit the androgen receptor hard, and they create the “granite” look
that defines a contest-ready physique.
But their power is a double-edged sword. While they harden the physique, they soften the
arteries. They tank HDL, spike LDL and ApoB, and hammer connective tissue integrity. The
athlete chasing a polished look must understand the trade-offs — and more importantly, how to
detect and mitigate them with precision bloodwork.

1) The DHT Mechanistic Signature
•
•
•

•
•

Non-Aromatizing: No direct conversion to estrogen → “dry” look.
Strong AR Affinity: High binding drives neural tone, aggression, and density.
SHBG Modulation:
o Winstrol → crashes SHBG, spiking free hormones.
o Proviron → binds SHBG, freeing testosterone.
o Masteron, Primo → moderate suppression.
Androgenic Without Progestogenic: Unlike 19-nors, no prolactin pathway issues.
C17-AA Status:
o Orals (Anavar, Winstrol, Superdrol) = hepatotoxic.
o Injectables (Primo, Mast) = liver-cleaner, lipid-toxic.

2) Compound Profiles
Primobolan (Methenolone)
•
•
•
•

Use: lean bulk, recomp, contest prep backbone.
Pros: Mild, clean, minimal water retention, stable labs.
Cons: Expensive, frequently faked, modest anabolic potency.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓ 5–15 mg/dL. LDL ↑ mildly.
o ALT/AST near baseline, GGT stable.
o HCT ↑ modestly.

Masteron (Drostanolone)
•
•
•
•

Use: Prep finisher, hardness amplifier, synergist with test.
Pros: Anti-estrogenic “feel,” improves look when lean.
Cons: Limited use outside prep; androgenic hair loss risk.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓ 10–25. LDL ↑.
o SHBG ↓ → higher free T/E.
o Total E2 may fall, free E2 less so.

Proviron (Mesterolone)
•
•
•
•

Use: Prep synergy, libido/mood boost, SHBG management.
Pros: Oral, not C17-AA, mild on liver.
Cons: Weak anabolic, poor mass builder.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓ 5–15. LDL ↑ mildly.
o Liver minimal stress.
o SHBG strongly ↓, free T ↑.

Anavar (Oxandrolone)
•
•
•
•

Use: Strength retention in deficit, female-friendly at low dose.
Pros: Strong AR action per mg, neurological drive.
Cons: Lipid collapse, not as “mild” as marketed.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓↓ (20–35). LDL ↑↑.
o ALT/AST ↑. GGT ↑ = true hepatotoxicity.

Winstrol (Stanozolol)
•
•
•
•

Use: Prep finisher, extreme dryness and vascularity.
Pros: Explosive strength, dry look, oral or injectable.
Cons: Worst lipid profile, tendon fragility, liver strain.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓↓ (<20). LDL ↑↑.
o ALT/AST ↑↑. GGT ↑.
o SHBG ↓↓↓.

o

BP ↑, resting HR ↑.

Superdrol (Methasterone)
•
•
•
•

Use: Short-term “nuclear option” for size/strength.
Pros: Massive scale and strength gains in weeks.
Cons: Severe hepatotoxicity, catastrophic lipid collapse.
Lab Signature:
o HDL → near 0. LDL ↑↑↑.
o ALT/AST 3–5× ULN.
o GGT ↑, bilirubin ↑, ALP ↑ → cholestasis risk.

Timeframe:
•
•
•

Orals: Lipids and liver markers shift in 2–4 weeks.
Injectables: Lipid shifts are slower (6–8 weeks), but cumulative.
Reversibility: 4–12 weeks off drug, plus interventions. Longer if repeated abuse.

4) Risks Beyond the Numbers
•
•
•
•

Cardiovascular: DHTs are the worst offenders for long-term atherosclerotic risk. ApoB
↑ + HDL collapse = endothelial strain.
Connective Tissue: Winstrol reduces collagen synthesis → tendon rupture risk when
combined with low E2 and prep fatigue.
Prostate: PSA can creep in older men; higher risk with chronic DHT load.
Mental State: Proviron/Mast often boost libido and mood; prolonged dryness/low E2
can backfire (depression, irritability).

5) Mitigation Protocols
Cardiovascular (Lipids & ApoB)
•
•
•

Lifestyle: Zone 2 cardio, HIIT 1–2x/week, soluble fiber (30–40 g/day), Mediterraneanstyle fats.
Supplements: Omega-3 (2–4 g EPA/DHA), citrus bergamot, psyllium husk.
Pharmaceutical Rescue (with clinician):
o Rosuvastatin (Reta): LDL ↓ up to 60%, HDL ↑ 5–10%. Slight HbA1c ↑ (~0.3–
0.4%).
o Pitavastatin: LDL ↓, HDL ↑, A1c neutral or improved (ideal if GH/insulin
present).
o Ezetimibe: Additive LDL lowering; no A1c effect.

Liver
•
•
•

Support: TUDCA, NAC, hydration, no alcohol.
Red Flags: ALT/AST >3× ULN + GGT ↑ = true hepatic stress.
Immediate Pull: Dark urine, jaundice, itching = cholestasis → stop orals immediately.

Hematology
•
•
•

Watch hematocrit, especially stacked with test.
Donation only if medically appropriate.
More cardio + hydration.

Connective Tissue
•
•
•

Don’t crash E2.
Periodize loads in prep, especially on Winstrol.
Collagen + vitamin C pre-training.

Prostate
•

Baseline PSA after age 35, monitor trends.

•

If PSA ↑ steadily → pull DHTs, urology referral.

6) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Over-Dried Prep
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 200 mg + Mast 400 mg + Proviron 50 mg, heavy AI.
Labs: E2 <10 pg/mL, HDL 32 → 28, LDL ↑ 20.
Symptoms: Joint pain, flat look, poor sleep.
Fix: Drop AI, reduce Proviron, E2 → 35 pg/mL. Conditioning held, joints recovered,
athlete finished prep sharper.

Case B — Winstrol Spiral
•
•
•
•

Stack: Low-test + Winstrol 50 mg/day, 6 weeks out.
Labs: HDL 18, LDL 178, ALT/AST ↑2.5× ULN, GGT borderline ↑.
Symptoms: BP ↑, tendon pain.
Fix: Drop Winstrol, add ezetimibe + citrus bergamot, 20 min more cardio/day. Replaced
with Masteron. HDL ↑ to 27, LDL ↓ to 134 in 3 weeks. Athlete peaked safely.

Case C — Superdrol Disaster
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 400 mg + SD 20 mg/day, 4 weeks.
Labs: HDL 12, LDL 200+, ALT/AST 4× ULN, GGT ↑, bilirubin ↑.
Symptoms: Dark urine, lethargy.
Fix: Immediate drug cessation, medical eval, liver support. Took 10 weeks to normalize
labs. No permanent damage, but close call.

7) The Takeaway
DHT derivatives are the polishers of bodybuilding. They sharpen lines, dry out physiques, and
add aggression in prep. But every milligram has a cost — and that cost is paid in lipid collapse,
connective tissue risk, and sometimes organ strain.
The enhanced athlete’s edge comes from knowing when to push them, when to drop them,
and how to mitigate their damage. Labs reveal the trade-offs; interventions like rosuvastatin
or pitavastatin extend career longevity; discipline ensures you don’t “win the look” but lose
your health.
Master DHTs, and you’ll carve a masterpiece. Misuse them, and you’ll carve your career short.

Chapter 6 — 19-Nors: The Titans of
Anabolism
Among all anabolic families, none have the reputation — or the risk profile — of the 19-nor
derivatives. Born from nandrolone (19-nortestosterone), these compounds are anabolic
sledgehammers. They can pack on mass, harden a physique, and transform an athlete’s look in
weeks. But their very power lies in mechanisms that disrupt balance: progestogenic activity,
prolactin elevation, kidney stress, blood pressure rise, insulin resistance, and severe
neurological side effects.
In bodybuilding, these are not casual compounds. They’re advanced tools for advanced athletes
— and without disciplined bloodwork, they are a shortcut to disaster.

1) The Core 19-Nors
Nandrolone Decanoate (“Deca”)
•
•
•
•

•

Profile: Long-ester injectable, used in medicine for anemia, cachexia, joint recovery.
Anabolic Strength: 125% of testosterone (on paper), androgenic 37%.
Best Use: Off-season mass, joint comfort, injury recovery (low dose).
Lab Signature:
o Prolactin ↑ mildly.
o Progesterone receptor stimulation.
o Lipids: HDL ↓ moderate, LDL ↑.
o Hematocrit ↑.
o Kidney stress → creatinine may appear elevated (true vs false signal: use cystatinC).
Risks: ED (“deca dick”), prolactin gyno, BP ↑, kidney strain.

Trenbolone (Enanthate / Acetate / Hex)
•
•
•

Profile: The most feared and worshiped compound in bodybuilding. No aromatization,
binds AR ~3–5× stronger than testosterone.
Best Use: Contest prep, recomp, lean bulk (only in advanced athletes).
Lab Signature:
o Prolactin ↑ (varies by user).
o SHBG ↓↓↓.
o Lipids: HDL collapse, LDL ↑.
o Kidney stress: BP ↑, proteinuria risk.
o Glucose: insulin resistance ↑; HbA1c rises if prolonged.

o
•

Liver: hepatotoxic only in oral/parabolan forms.

Risks:
o
o
o
o

Severe cardiovascular strain (BP, LV hypertrophy).
Neurological: insomnia, aggression, anxiety (“tren cough,” “tren paranoia”).
Renal: creeping kidney decline, proteinuria.
Endocrine: prolactin gyno + ED.

Trestolone (Ment)
•
•
•

•

•

Profile: 7α-methyl-19-nortestosterone. Potent anabolic + androgenic.
Best Use: Rarely used; considered “research-only” or exotic.
Lab Signature:
o Prolactin ↑↑.
o Estrogen ↑ (Ment aromatizes heavily).
o Lipids: HDL ↓, LDL ↑ (severe).
o SHBG ↓↓↓.
Risks:
o “Double-hit” of estrogenic and progestogenic activity = uncontrollable gyno.
o Severe suppression of natural axis.
o Extreme BP and lipid strain.
Field View: A physique “nuke.” Rapid size, rapid damage.

2) Mechanisms of Risk
Prolactin & Progesterone Pathways
•
•
•
•

•

All 19-nors activate the progesterone receptor.
This elevates prolactin indirectly (dopamine inhibition).
Symptoms: ED, low libido, gynecomastia, mood swings.
Bloodwork:
o Prolactin: >25 ng/mL + symptoms = red flag.
o Progesterone: elevated vs baseline.
Fixes: Cabergoline or pramipexole (dopamine agonists) — but use sparingly. Overuse
causes cardiac valve issues.

Kidney Stress
•

Tren especially is nephrotoxic: raises BP, induces vascular stiffness, increases
proteinuria.

•

Labs to Watch:
o Creatinine (falsely elevated by muscle mass).
o Cystatin-C (better athlete marker).
o eGFR (may appear lower than true in lifters).
o Urinalysis (protein, microalbumin = true stress signal).

Blood Pressure & Cardiovascular
•
•
•

•

Tren causes vasoconstriction and sodium retention → BP elevation.
Nandrolone increases hematocrit → thicker blood.
Labs to Watch:
o BP daily (home cuff).
o Hematocrit, hemoglobin.
o ApoB, LDL-P.
Fixes: More cardio, ARBs (telmisartan), lower dose, donations if HCT >55%.

insulin Sensitivity
•
•

•

19-nors impair glucose disposal agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent agent. Tren especially reduces GLUT4 activity.
Labs to Watch:
o Fasting glucose.
o HbA1c.
o Fasting insulin / C-peptide.
Fixes: Cardio, metformin, berberine, lower GH use.

Neurological & Psychological
•
•
•
•

Tren alters dopamine & GABA signaling.
Symptoms: insomnia, paranoia, aggression, depression post-use.
Labs to Watch: None direct — but prolactin elevation often coincides.
Fixes: Sleep hygiene, taper caffeine, melatonin/5-HTP, reduce dose.

3) Field-Ready Chart: 19-Nors Risks & Labs

4) Mitigation Protocols
Cardio & BP
•
•
•

Minimum 150–300 min/week zone 2 cardio.
Monitor BP daily.
If >135/85 consistently → ARB (telmisartan preferred; clinician-guided).

Lipids
•
•

Omega-3, citrus bergamot, fiber.
If HDL <25 and LDL >160:
o Rosuvastatin (Reta): maximum LDL reduction, HDL ↑ slightly, but watch
HbA1c.
o Pitavastatin: lipid rescue without glycemic penalty (ideal if running GH/insulin).
o Ezetimibe stack if needed.

Kidneys
•
•
•

Use cystatin-C + urinalysis, not just creatinine.
Stay hydrated, moderate sodium intake.
Pull 19-nor if proteinuria emerges.

Prolactin
•
•

Use dopamine agonists only when symptomatic + prolactin elevated.
Avoid “preventative” cabergoline — risk > benefit.

Psychological
•
•
•

Respect sleep. Tren-induced insomnia ruins prep.
Reduce stimulants.
Track mood changes — if paranoia sets in, it’s not “grit,” it’s toxicity.

5) Case Vignettes

Case A — The Deca Off-Season
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 300 mg + Deca 400 mg.
Labs: HDL ↓ 15, LDL ↑ 20, prolactin ↑ 28, creatinine ↑ falsely. Cystatin-C normal.
Symptoms: Libido ↓, mild ED.
Fix: Lower Deca to 200 mg, added Proviron (free T boost, SHBG ↓), prolactin
normalized, libido restored.

Case B — The Tren Prep Spiral
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 200 mg + Tren A 400 mg + Mast 400 mg.
Labs (6 weeks): HDL 21, LDL 160, ApoB high, BP 145/90, A1c ↑ 5.7%.
Symptoms: Insomnia, anxiety, night sweats.
Fix: Dropped Tren to 200 mg, added telmisartan + bergamot, added pitavastatin (lipid
rescue without worsening A1c). Labs improved, athlete peaked.

Case C — The Ment Monster
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 250 mg + Ment 50 mg/day.
Labs (4 weeks): Prolactin ↑ 45, estradiol ↑ 80 pg/mL, HDL 18, LDL 190.
Symptoms: Gyno flare, BP ↑.
Fix: Dropped Ment immediately, cabergoline short course, statin therapy. Took 8 weeks
for labs to normalize. Athlete refused to touch Ment again.

6) The Takeaway
The 19-nors are the double-edged swords of bodybuilding. They give size, strength, hardness,
and aggression — but they demand a steep tax on cardiovascular, renal, endocrine, and mental
health.
You can’t “hack” Tren or Deca. You can only monitor, mitigate, and respect their risks. That
means:
•
•
•
•

Bloodwork every 4–6 weeks.
Tracking BP, prolactin, ApoB, cystatin-C.
Using statin therapy when lipids collapse.
Pulling back when kidneys or the mind show red flags.

The greats aren’t the ones who ran the most Tren — they’re the ones who learned how to walk
the line without falling off the cliff.

Chapter 7 — Oral Anabolics & Exotics: Fast
Gains, Faster Strain
Oral anabolics are the fastest-acting PEDs in bodybuilding. They deliver rapid strength, scale
weight, and visual changes that injectable cycles simply can’t match in the short term. They’re
used for kickstarts, contest finishers, and aggressive bulks.
But the convenience of swallowing a pill comes with the steepest trade-offs. Nearly all orals are
17α-alkylated (C17-AA) steroids, modified to survive first-pass liver metabolism. That same
modification is what makes them hepatotoxic and brutally damaging to lipids.
Orals are the equivalent of red-lining an engine: they make you go fast, but the wear is real, and
the labs always reveal it.

1) The Big Four Classics
Anadrol (Oxymetholone)
•
•
•

•

Profile: One of the most powerful bulking orals. Massive water, glycogen, and strength
increase.
Best Use: Short-term size blast or pre-contest fullness (with diuretics managed carefully).
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓↓↓ (often single digits). LDL ↑↑.
o ALT/AST ↑↑. GGT ↑ = true hepatic stress.
o Hematocrit ↑ via EPO stimulation.
Risks: BP ↑ from water + RBC, liver strain, worst lipid collapse in the class.

Dianabol (Methandrostenolone)
•
•
•

•

Profile: The original “mass builder.” Rapid size and strength, aromatizes strongly.
Best Use: Bulking kickstart.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓↓. LDL ↑.
o ALT/AST ↑ (training overlap possible).
o E2 ↑ from aromatization (bloat, gyno risk).
Risks: Estrogenic side effects, liver strain, lipid shifts.

Turinabol (Chlorodehydromethyltestosterone)
•
•
•

•

Profile: East German “Olympic steroid.” Lean, dry gains; less water retention than
Dianabol.
Best Use: lean bulk, recomps, strength phases.
Lab Signature:
o HDL ↓↓, LDL ↑↑.
o ALT/AST ↑ moderately; GGT often ↑.
Risks: Still hepatotoxic, still brutal on cholesterol. Less aromatization, but not “mild.”

Halotestin (Fluoxymesterone)
•
•
•

•

Profile: The aggression drug. Little size, insane strength and neural drive. Contest-day
weapon.
Best Use: Short-term contest prep (1–3 weeks max).
Lab Signature:
o HDL collapse (often single digits).
o LDL ↑ sharply.
o ALT/AST ↑↑; GGT ↑↑.
Risks: Severe hepatotoxicity, psychological volatility, BP spikes.

2) Exotic Orals & Designer Steroids
•
•
•
•

Superdrol (Methasterone): Mentioned in Chapter 5. Nuclear option — rapid gains,
catastrophic labs.
Epistane / Havoc (Epitiostanol derivatives): Dry, hard look. Still C17-AA. Lipid
wreckers.
Mibolerone (“Cheque Drops”): Pre-fight aggression drug, not for physique building.
Highly hepatotoxic, rarely used in modern bodybuilding.
Designer blends (various “research” orals): Poorly studied, unpredictable, often worse
for lipids/liver than the classics.

Field note: The “exotic hype” orals almost always carry equal or greater risk than the Big
Four. Stick to known compounds if you’re already playing with fire.

3) The Lab Fingerprint of Orals
•

•

•
•
•

Lipid Collapse
o HDL often falls into single digits.
o LDL skyrockets.
o ApoB/LDL-P: elevated, vascular strain accelerates.
Liver Enzyme Spikes
o ALT/AST ↑. But remember: training can also elevate these.
o GGT ↑ = true hepatotoxicity.
o Bilirubin ↑, ALP ↑ = cholestasis (blocked bile flow).
Hematology
o Some orals (Anadrol, dbol) ↑ hematocrit via EPO stimulation → clot risk.
Blood Pressure
o Anadrol especially: water retention + hematocrit ↑ = hypertensive risk.
Glucose Control
o Orals are neutral-to-negative for insulin sensitivity. Add GH/insulin, and A1c can
creep upward.

4) Magnitude of Damage: Time Course

Notable Risk

Timeframe:
•
•
•

Changes show up in 2–3 weeks for most orals.
HDL recovery post-cycle: 4–8 weeks (if no permanent vascular change).
GGT normalization: variable, 2–12 weeks.

5) Risk Mitigation for Orals
Lipid Rescue
•
•

Lifestyle: Cardio, fiber, omega-3s, Mediterranean-style fats.
Supplements: Citrus bergamot, psyllium husk, plant sterols.

•

Pharmaceuticals (with clinician):
o Rosuvastatin (Reta): Most effective LDL-lowering, slight A1c ↑ (~0.3–0.4%).
o Pitavastatin: LDL reduction with HDL support, neutral or ↓ A1c (ideal if on
GH/insulin).
o Ezetimibe: Stack for extra LDL lowering.

Rule: Rosuvastatin for max rescue; pitavastatin if glucose sensitivity is already strained.

Liver Support
•
•
•
•

TUDCA (250–1,000 mg/day) → bile acid support.
NAC (600–1,200 mg/day) → glutathione support.
Zero alcohol, Tylenol, or NSAID stacking during oral runs.
Immediate pull if:
o ALT/AST >3× ULN + GGT ↑.
o Bilirubin ↑ with dark urine = cholestasis emergency.

Hematology
•
•

Anadrol/dbol: monitor hematocrit.
If >55% → consider phlebotomy/donation with clinician oversight.

Blood Pressure
•
•

Anadrol/Winstrol users should log daily BP.
ARBs (telmisartan) are the go-to intervention if needed.

6) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Anadrol Mass Blast
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 500 + Deca 400 + Anadrol 50 mg/day.
Labs (4 weeks): HDL 11, LDL 175, HCT 56%, ALT/AST ↑2.5× ULN, GGT borderline
↑.
Symptoms: BP 150/95, headaches.
Fix: Pull Anadrol, add telmisartan, rosuvastatin, cardio +30 min/day. Within 6 weeks:
HDL ↑ to 29, LDL ↓ to 120, BP normalized.

Case B — The dbol Classic
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 400 + dbol 30 mg/day.
Labs (6 weeks): HDL ↓ from 42 → 20, LDL ↑ 40, ALT ↑ 2× ULN, E2 ↑ 70 pg/mL.
Symptoms: Bloat, gyno onset.
Fix: Adjust AI dosing, drop dbol after 8 weeks. Added pitavastatin for lipids. Labs
normalized in 5 weeks.

Case C — The Halo Contest Week
•
•
•
•

Stack: Low-test + Mast + Halo 20 mg/day (10 days out).
Labs (2 weeks): HDL 9, LDL 170, ALT/AST ↑3×, GGT ↑, bilirubin ↑ borderline.
Symptoms: Aggression, irritability, yellow tinge to eyes.
Fix: Immediate pull. Short course, labs normalized in ~8 weeks. Athlete still looked
sharp on stage but vowed never again.

7) The Takeaway
Oral anabolics are short-term weapons, not long-term tools. They are best reserved for:
•
•
•

Kickstarting a mass phase (dbol, Anadrol).
Contest finishing (Halo, Winstrol).
Strategic lean bulks or recomps (Anavar, tbol).

But the cost is always there: lipid implosion, liver strain, BP spikes, and hematology shifts.
The enhanced athlete’s rule: run them short, pull frequent labs, and use pharmaceutical
rescue (statins, ezetimibe, TUDCA, ARBs) when needed. Ignore the labs, and the orals that
give you the fastest results will also give you the fastest exit from bodybuilding.

Chapter 8 — Growth Hormone & IGF-1:
Beyond Muscle, Into Metabolism
Growth hormone (GH) has been called the “fountain of youth,” the “ultimate fat burner,” and the
“secret weapon” of bodybuilding. Reality? It’s none of those things in isolation — but used
properly, it becomes one of the most effective synergists in the enhanced athlete’s arsenal.
GH changes the way your body partitions nutrients, mobilizes fat, and maintains fullness in
contest prep. Its metabolite, IGF-1 (insulin-Like Growth Factor 1), is where much of the
anabolic drive comes from. Together, GH and IGF-1 create a platform that magnifies the effect
of anabolics, insulin, and diet.
But GH is also misunderstood — it can cause insulin resistance, organ growth, sleep apnea,
and even fuel cancer progression if abused. That’s why precision bloodwork is non-negotiable.
GH isn’t just “plug and play.” It requires measurement, adjustment, and synergy.

1) Growth Hormone Physiology in the enhanced athlete
•
•

•

Secretory Pattern: GH is pulsed naturally (peaks during sleep, fasting, hypoglycemia).
Injections flatten this rhythm, creating a “pharma profile.”
Primary Actions:
o Lipolysis: GH is a powerful fat mobilizer.
o Anti-Catabolic: GH preserves muscle during caloric deficit.
o Synergistic with insulin & Steroids: GH by itself builds modest muscle.
Combined with insulin + androgens = hyper-anabolism.
Downstream Mediator: Most GH effects come via IGF-1, produced in the liver
(measurable in serum).

2) Bloodwork Markers for GH/IGF-1
•

•

•

IGF-1: Primary marker of GH activity.
o Normal range: 100–300 ng/mL (age-dependent).
o Enhanced athletes often run 300–500+ ng/mL.
o Above ~600 ng/mL chronically = red flag (organ/tumor risk).
Fasting Glucose & HbA1c:
o GH antagonizes insulin → fasting glucose rises, A1c creeps upward.
o GH users often show A1c 5.6–6.0 (pre-diabetic range).
C-Peptide / Fasting insulin:
o Tracks pancreatic response to GH-induced resistance.

•
•

Lipids:
o GH can worsen HDL/LDL when combined with orals or DHTs.
Cancer Markers (if at risk):
o PSA (men >35).
o AFP, CEA in specific contexts. GH isn’t a carcinogen, but it can accelerate
existing growth.

3) Laboratory Kinetics of GH Use
•

•

•

Within 2 weeks:
o IGF-1 ↑ significantly.
o Fasting glucose ↑.
Within 4–8 weeks:
o A1c ↑ by 0.2–0.4 points.
o Lipid strain ↑ (esp. with oral/DHT stacks).
Long-term (>6 months):
o Organ growth (heart, intestines, kidneys) in high doses.
o Sleep apnea risk from soft tissue thickening.

4) Risks & Red Flags
•

•
•
•

insulin Resistance: Most common and most damaging if ignored.
o Symptoms: fatigue, carb “spillover,” stubborn fat loss.
o Labs: glucose >100 fasting, A1c >5.6.
Organ Stress: Chronic high IGF-1 pushes tissue growth beyond muscle (visceral,
cardiac).
Water Retention & Carpal Tunnel: Often dose-related; signal of overshooting.
Cancer Acceleration: GH doesn’t cause cancer but can fuel existing malignancies.

5) Synergy With Other Compounds
•
•
•
•

GH + Testosterone: Increases lean mass retention in deficit.
GH + insulin: Creates the “anabolic stack.” GH mobilizes fat, insulin drives nutrients
into muscle. Without insulin, GH is mostly anti-catabolic.
GH + DHTs (Primo/Mast/Var): Drives fat loss + hardness synergy.
GH + GLP-1 agonists (Semaglutide/Tirzepatide): Protects insulin sensitivity while
using GH — new frontier of bodybuilding synergy.

6) Practical Application (Educational)
•

•

•

Dosing Patterns:
o 2–4 IU/day = anti-catabolic, fat loss, contest prep.
o 4–6 IU/day = synergistic muscle growth with insulin/steroids.
o 8 IU/day = advanced use, high risk of resistance/organ growth.
Timing:
o Morning fasted (fat loss).
o Pre-training (synergy with insulin).
o Split dosing (reduce sides).
Cycle Duration:
o GH is long game. Real physique changes show in 16–20+ weeks.
o Not a “blast drug” like orals.

7) Mitigation Strategies
insulin Sensitivity
•
•
•
•

Labs: Glucose, A1c, C-peptide every 8 weeks.
Lifestyle: 150–300 min/week cardio, carb timing, fiber intake.
Supplements: Berberine (500 mg 2–3×/day), ALA, chromium.
Pharma (clinician-guided): Metformin (500–1000 mg), GLP-1 agonists
(Semaglutide/Tirzepatide).

Lipids
•

Combine GH with statin/ezetimibe if lipids crash (esp. with orals).

Cancer Vigilance
•
•

Screen based on age, family history.
Keep IGF-1 <500–550 ng/mL long-term.

8) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Off-Season GH User
•
•

Stack: Test 400 + Deca 300 + GH 4 IU/day.
Labs (12 weeks): IGF-1 ↑ to 380 (baseline 220), fasting glucose ↑ to 104, A1c ↑ from 5.2
→ 5.7.

•

Fix: Added 500 mg berberine 3×/day, 20 more min cardio daily. At 8 weeks, glucose ↓ to
94, A1c stabilized at 5.5.

Case B — The Contest Prep GH Push
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 200 + Mast 400 + Tren 300 + GH 6 IU split dose.
Labs (8 weeks): IGF-1 480, HDL ↓ 18, LDL ↑ 170, glucose 102.
Symptoms: Carpal tunnel, sleep disruption.
Fix: Reduced GH to 4 IU, added pitavastatin + ezetimibe. Symptoms resolved, lipids
normalized, IGF-1 stayed ~350.

Case C — The Reckless High Dose
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 600 + Tren 400 + GH 12 IU/day + insulin.
Labs (16 weeks): IGF-1 >600, A1c ↑ 6.1, fasting glucose 118, creatinine ↑ borderline.
Symptoms: Severe water retention, constant fatigue, borderline hypertensive.
Fix: Cycle pulled immediately. Athlete required 10+ weeks to restore insulin sensitivity.
Long-term unknowns remain.

9) Field Chart — GH & IGF-1 in Bodybuilding
Dose
(IU/day)

10) The Takeaway
Growth hormone is not a magic pill. It’s a synergist, not a soloist. Its real power comes from
combining with insulin, androgens, and precision nutrition.

But GH use without bloodwork is a blind gamble. IGF-1, glucose, A1c, and lipids must be
tracked every 8–12 weeks. Without that feedback loop, what looks like a fat-burning, musclesparing tool can quietly turn into insulin resistance, cardiovascular risk, or organ strain.
Used wisely, GH is a career extender. Used recklessly, it’s a fast track to long-term
metabolic dysfunction.

ChatGPT said:

Perfect — let’s tackle Chapter 9 — insulin in Bodybuilding, which is arguably the most
misunderstood and dangerous PED in the sport.
Some lifters think of it as the “ultimate anabolic,” others fear it like poison. The truth is —
insulin is neither a magic bullet nor a death sentence. It’s a tool, and like all powerful tools, it
cuts both ways. When paired with GH and androgens, insulin becomes one of the most potent
growth drivers in physique transformation. But without precision — bloodwork, diet control, and
monitoring — it’s a recipe for fat gain, hypoglycemia, or long-term diabetes.
Here’s the fully developed long-form Chapter 9 for Precision Bloodwork and Physique
Transformation for Enhanced Bodybuilders:

Chapter 9 — insulin in Bodybuilding: The
Double-Edged Anabolic
insulin is one of the most anabolic hormones in the human body. Unlike testosterone or growth
hormone, insulin isn’t just permissive — it’s decisive. It determines whether nutrients are stored
in muscle or fat, whether recovery is maximized or blunted, and whether GH use leads to
growth or to insulin resistance.
Bodybuilders discovered decades ago that combining testosterone, GH, and insulin creates an
“anabolic triad” capable of muscle gain beyond what any single agent can produce. This is the
stack behind many of the freakiest physiques in bodybuilding history.
But the risks are real: hypoglycemia, fat spillover, and long-term beta-cell burnout if abused.
insulin is not a casual PED. It requires discipline, diet structure, and blood glucose
monitoring — without exception.

1) Physiology Refresher: Why insulin Matters
•
•
•
•

Primary Role: Drives glucose and amino acids into muscle (glycogen + protein
synthesis).
Anti-Catabolic: Suppresses protein breakdown.
Synergy with GH: GH mobilizes fat but increases insulin resistance. insulin restores
sensitivity and maximizes nutrient uptake.
Storage Hormone: insulin doesn’t care if calories go to muscle or fat — it depends
entirely on diet and timing.

2) Bloodwork for insulin Use
•
•
•
•
•

Fasting Glucose: Normal 70–99 mg/dL. >100 = impaired fasting glucose.
HbA1c: Long-term glucose control. <5.6 = normal; 5.7–6.4 = pre-diabetic.
C-Peptide: Measures pancreatic insulin production.
Fasting insulin: Used with glucose to estimate HOMA-IR (insulin resistance).
Lipids: Poor insulin control = high triglycerides, low HDL.

Precision athletes monitor glucose daily (glucometer/CGM) and pull labs every 8–12 weeks.

3) Types of insulin Used in Bodybuilding

•

•

•

Rapid-Acting (Humalog, Novolog):
o Onset: 10–20 min.
o Peak: 1–2 hrs.
o Duration: 3–5 hrs.
o Best Use: Pre- or post-workout with controlled carb intake.
Short-Acting (Regular “R”):
o Onset: 30–60 min.
o Duration: 6–8 hrs.
o Best Use: Old-school method; less common today.
Long-Acting (Lantus, Levemir):
o Flat 12–24 hr release.
o Best Use: Background anabolic support in advanced protocols.
o Risk: Higher fat storage risk if diet isn’t perfect.

4) Laboratory Effects of insulin Misuse
•

•

•
•

Hypoglycemia Episodes:
o Immediate danger: confusion, sweating, fainting, seizures.
o Glucose <60 mg/dL = red flag; <40 = emergency.
Long-Term Resistance:
o HbA1c creeps above 5.6 → 5.9 → 6.2.
o Fasting insulin ↑; pancreas under strain.
Lipid Changes:
o Poor insulin sensitivity → high triglycerides, low HDL.
Kidneys:
o Chronic insulin resistance → early nephropathy risk.

5) Common Mistakes Athletes Make
1. “Carb dumping” — Slamming 100–200 g sugar post-shot. → Fat gain, poor nutrient
partitioning.
2. Using insulin without GH. → Drives fat storage more than muscle growth.
3. No glucose monitoring. → Hypo roulette.
4. Stacking with orals/DHTs without statins. → Lipid suicide.
5. Treating it as a “blast” drug. → insulin works best moderate + consistent, not
extreme.

6) Precision insulin Use (Educational)
•
•
•

When: Pre-training (with controlled carbs + EAAs), post-training (glycogen
replenishment), or steady-state background (Lantus).
Why: To enhance GH synergy, glycogen storage, and amino acid uptake.
How: Only with planned macros and blood glucose monitoring.

Rule: Never pin insulin without carbs on hand + a glucometer nearby.

7) Risk Mitigation Protocols
Daily Monitoring
•
•

Finger-stick glucose or continuous glucose monitor (CGM).
Keep fasted glucose <100 mg/dL.

Bloodwork
•
•

HbA1c every 8–12 weeks.
C-peptide + fasting insulin every 3–6 months.

Nutritional Control
•
•

Use insulin only when carb intake is predictable and structured.
Pair carbs with protein + minimal fat to enhance muscle uptake.

Cardio & Sensitivity
•
•

Cardio enhances GLUT4 → improved insulin sensitivity.
Supplements: Berberine (500 mg 2–3×/day), ALA, chromium.

Pharmaceutical Support (clinician-guided)
•
•

Metformin: Improves insulin sensitivity, lowers hepatic glucose output.
GLP-1/GIP Agonists (Semaglutide, Tirzepatide): Powerful modern tools to preserve
insulin sensitivity during GH+insulin use.

8) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Overeager Bulker
•
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 600 + dbol + insulin 10 IU post-training.
Protocol: 150 g sugar post-shot.
Labs (12 weeks): HbA1c ↑ 5.2 → 6.0, triglycerides ↑, HDL ↓ 20.
Symptoms: Fat gain, lethargy.
Fix: Dropped insulin, added berberine, restored diet structure. Within 8 weeks, A1c ↓ to
5.6.

Case B — The GH+insulin Synergy
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 300 + GH 4 IU/day + Humalog 6 IU pre-workout.
Protocol: 50 g carbs + EAAs pre-training, 60 g carbs + whey post-training.
Labs (16 weeks): IGF-1 ↑ 360 → 480, A1c stable 5.4, fasting glucose 92.
Outcome: lean bulk success, labs within range.

Case C — The Long-Acting Trap
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 400 + Deca 300 + GH 6 IU/day + Lantus 20 IU.
Labs (10 weeks): A1c ↑ 6.2, triglycerides ↑ 200, HDL ↓ 18.
Symptoms: Rapid fat gain, water retention, fatigue.
Fix: Pulled Lantus, added cardio + pitavastatin. A1c ↓ to 5.8 in 12 weeks, but fat gain
remained. Lesson: long-acting insulin is not for casual use.

10) The Takeaway
insulin is bodybuilding’s most powerful nutrient partitioner. It can make GH effective, refill
glycogen like nothing else, and turn a clean diet into pure muscle. But it is also the fastest path
to fat gain, hypoglycemia, and pre-diabetes if misused.
The disciplined athlete uses insulin with:
•
•
•
•

Blood glucose monitoring daily.
Structured carb timing.
Regular labs (A1c, C-peptide, insulin).
Synergy with GH and training.

Without those controls, insulin is not an advantage — it’s a liability. With them, it becomes a
precision scalpel for growth.

Chapter 10 — GLP-1, GIP & Multi-Agonist
Compounds: The New Era of Metabolic
Control
Bodybuilding has always borrowed from medicine. Just as testosterone moved from HRT to
enhancement, and GH from pediatric deficiency to mass monster fuel, the new class of incretin
mimetics — GLP-1, GIP, and dual/triple agonists — are crossing over.
Why? Because the very problems that plague enhanced athletes — insulin resistance, lipid
collapse, appetite control, and blood glucose drift from GH/insulin abuse — are exactly the
problems these drugs were designed to fix.
For the serious enhanced bodybuilder, these compounds are not just fat-loss aids. They may
become the cornerstones of longevity protocols, keeping athletes on the stage — and alive —
for decades longer than the reckless cycles of the past.

1) Mechanisms of GLP-1 & GIP Agonists
•

•

•

GLP-1 (Glucagon-Like Peptide-1):
o Increases insulin secretion (glucose-dependent).
o Suppresses glucagon release.
o Slows gastric emptying (↑ satiety).
o Improves beta-cell function.
GIP (Glucose-Dependent Insulinotropic Polypeptide):
o Potentiates insulin secretion.
o Improves fat metabolism, synergizes with GLP-1.
Multi-Agonists (Tirzepatide, Retatrutide):
o Combine GLP-1 + GIP (and sometimes glucagon).
o Show superior weight loss, glucose control, and lipid improvements over
GLP-1 alone.

Translation for bodybuilders: These compounds shield you from the metabolic fallout of
GH, insulin, and heavy oral cycles.

2) Clinical Evidence Relevant to Bodybuilding
•

•

•

STEP-4 Trial (Semaglutide):
o Showed weight loss plateaus at ~24 weeks, but resets after 4–6 week break.
o Implication: cycling GLP-1s may be optimal for enhanced athletes.
SURPASS Trials (Tirzepatide):
o Superior glucose control vs semaglutide.
o More profound A1c reductions (−2.0% vs −1.5%).
o Greater lipid improvements.
Retatrutide (triple agonist, in trials):
o 24% average weight loss in non-diabetics.
o Improved insulin sensitivity + lipid panels beyond GLP-1/GIP.

3) Bloodwork Impact
Improvements Seen in Labs:
•
•
•
•
•

HbA1c ↓ 0.5–2.0% (dose-dependent).
Fasting glucose ↓.
Triglycerides ↓ 20–30%.
HDL ↑ modestly.
LDL ↓ slightly.

Neutral/Negative Effects:
•
•
•

GI side effects (nausea, vomiting, slowed gastric emptying).
Rare pancreatitis (watch amylase/lipase).
Thyroid C-cell tumors in rodents (uncertain in humans).

4) Practical Application in Bodybuilding (Educational)
•

•

•

When to Use:
o During GH/insulin stacks → protect insulin sensitivity.
o During off-season → prevent fat spillover.
o During contest prep → appetite suppression, improved lipid control.
Dosing Context:
o Semaglutide: 0.25 → 2.4 mg weekly (titrated).
o Tirzepatide: 2.5 → 15 mg weekly.
o Retatrutide: still experimental, not field-available.
Cycle Theory (based on STEP-4):
o Run 16–24 weeks → plateau.

o

Take 4–6 week break → reset sensitivity.

5) Risk Mitigation & Monitoring
•

•

Labs to Monitor:
o HbA1c every 8–12 weeks.
o Lipid panel every 8–12 weeks.
o Amylase, lipase (pancreatic enzymes).
o Thyroid labs (if strong family history of thyroid cancer).
Lifestyle Integration:
o Hydration + fiber = minimize GI issues.
o Structured meal timing (slowed digestion can complicate peri-workout nutrition).

6) Case Vignettes
Case A — The GH User with Rising A1c
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 300 + GH 6 IU/day + Humalog 6 IU pre/post.
Labs (16 weeks): A1c ↑ 5.3 → 5.9, triglycerides ↑ 180.
Intervention: Added semaglutide 0.5 → 1 mg weekly.
Labs (12 weeks later): A1c ↓ 5.6, triglycerides ↓ 130. Glucose stability improved.

Case B — The Off-Season Bulk with Appetite Control
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 500 + Deca 400 + GH 4 IU/day.
Problem: Constant overeating, fat gain outpacing muscle.
Fix: Added tirzepatide 5 mg weekly.
Result: Caloric intake dropped 400 kcal/day naturally, fat gain slowed, lean mass
continued rising.

Case C — The Contest Prep Plateau
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 200 + Tren 300 + Mast 400 + GH 6 IU/day.
Issue: Fat loss slowed despite deficit.
Intervention: Added semaglutide 1 mg weekly.
Outcome: Appetite suppression, easier adherence, triglycerides dropped, stage
conditioning improved.

8) The Takeaway
GLP-1 and GIP agonists are the modern counterbalance to the chaos of GH, insulin, and oralheavy stacks. They restore insulin sensitivity, protect lipids, and suppress runaway appetite — all
while supporting the exact blood markers enhanced athletes need to control.
These drugs will not build muscle directly, but they can make the anabolic environment
cleaner, safer, and more sustainable.
The old-school equation was:
Test + GH + insulin = Growth (but with risk).
The modern equation may soon be:
Test + GH + insulin + GLP-1 = Growth + Control.
That control could mean the difference between 5 years of reckless size and 20 years of
sustainable performance.

Chapter 10 — GLP-1, GIP & Multi-Agonist
Compounds: The New Era of Metabolic
Control
Bodybuilding has always borrowed from medicine. Testosterone, growth hormone, and insulin
all came from medical necessity and found their way into the arsenal of physique athletes.
Today, the next class of drugs crossing that boundary are incretin-based therapies: GLP-1
receptor agonists, GIP co-agonists, and now multi-agonists like tirzepatide and retatrutide.
For the enhanced bodybuilder, these aren’t just fat-loss tools. They may represent the missing
piece in the longevity puzzle — a way to run GH, insulin, and heavy cycles while keeping
glucose, lipids, and appetite under control.
In short: these are not mass builders, but they can make every anabolic in your stack work
cleaner, safer, and longer.

1) Mechanisms of GLP-1 & GIP Agonists
•

•

•

•

GLP-1 (Glucagon-Like Peptide-1):
o Stimulates glucose-dependent insulin release.
o Suppresses glucagon (less hepatic glucose output).
o Slows gastric emptying → satiety, appetite control.
o Improves beta-cell preservation.
GIP (Glucose-Dependent Insulinotropic Polypeptide):
o Potentiates insulin release.
o Enhances fat metabolism.
o Works synergistically with GLP-1.
Glucagon Receptor Agonism (Retatrutide):
o Promotes fat oxidation and energy expenditure.
o Balances GLP-1’s appetite suppression with metabolic drive.
Amylin (Cagrilintide):
o Satiety peptide normally co-secreted with insulin.
o Adds powerful appetite suppression + blood sugar stabilization.

Translation for bodybuilders: GLP-1s and their cousins fix the exact metabolic wreckage
GH, insulin, and orals create — insulin resistance, high triglycerides, appetite dysregulation,
and creeping HbA1c.

2) Clinical Evidence Relevant to Bodybuilding
•

•

•

•

STEP-4 Trial (Semaglutide):
o Showed weight loss plateaus at ~24 weeks.
o Reset after a 4–6 week break → suggests cycling may be ideal for athletes.
SURPASS Trials (Tirzepatide):
o Superior glucose + A1c reductions vs semaglutide.
o More profound triglyceride lowering.
Retatrutide (triple agonist, Phase 3):
o 24% weight loss in non-diabetics.
o Improved insulin sensitivity, A1c, triglycerides.
Semaglutide + Cagrilintide (Dual Therapy):
o Deeper appetite suppression + glucose control than sema alone.
o Represents the “real-world proto-quint” stack.

3) Bloodwork Impact
Positive Shifts:
•
•
•
•
•

HbA1c ↓ 0.5–2.0%.
Fasting glucose ↓.
Triglycerides ↓ 20–40%.
HDL ↑ modestly.
LDL ↓ slightly.

Risks / Caveats:
•
•
•
•

GI side effects (nausea, vomiting, delayed gastric emptying).
Rare pancreatitis (track amylase, lipase).
Possible thyroid C-cell issues in rodents (uncertain in humans).
Slowed digestion can complicate peri-workout nutrition.

4) Application in Bodybuilding
•
•
•
•

Off-Season: Prevent fat spillover during surplus. Appetite suppression helps hold cleaner
weight.
GH + insulin Stacks: Counteract GH-induced resistance, stabilize glucose.
Contest Prep: Suppress hunger, improve lipid panels, hold performance deeper into a
deficit.
Post-Cycle Recovery: Maintain weight control and improve metabolic labs after a blast.

Dosing Ranges (Educational):
•
•
•
•

Semaglutide: 0.25 → 2.4 mg weekly.
Tirzepatide: 2.5 → 15 mg weekly.
Retatrutide: Still investigational.
Cagrilintide: Currently trial-only.

5) Risk Mitigation & Monitoring
Labs to Track:
•
•
•
•

HbA1c every 8–12 weeks.
Lipid panel every 8–12 weeks.
Amylase, lipase (pancreatic health).
Thyroid labs if family history of thyroid issues.

Lifestyle Adjustments:
•
•
•

Extra hydration + fiber → reduce GI side effects.
Space peri-workout nutrition (fast digestion shakes may sit longer).
Start with low doses → titrate slowly.

6) Case Vignettes

8) Quad- and Quint-Agonist Stacking in Bodybuilding
Pharma’s future lies in quad- and quint-agonist molecules. Enhanced athletes can already
mimic this by stacking GH, GLP-1/GIP agents, and amylin analogues.

Retatrutide (triple agonist):
•

Hits GLP-1 + GIP + glucagon.

Quad-Agonist Options (4 Pathways Active):
•
•
•

Retatrutide + Cagrilintide: GLP-1, GIP, glucagon, amylin. (No GH/IGF axis.)
Retatrutide + GH: GLP-1, GIP, glucagon, IGF-1 axis. (No amylin.)
Tirzepatide + GH + Cagrilintide: GLP-1, GIP, IGF-1 axis, amylin. (No glucagon.)

Quint-Agonist Stack (All 5 Pathways Active):
•
•
•

Retatrutide + GH + Cagrilintide.
Covers: GLP-1, GIP, glucagon, amylin, IGF-1 axis.
Closest thing to a pharma “holy grail” quint agonist available today.

Lab & Performance Outcomes
•
•
•
•
•
•

HbA1c: Stable or ↓ under GH/insulin.
TG: ↓ 25–40%.
HDL: ↑ consistently.
IGF-1: ↑ via GH.
Appetite: Strong suppression (amylin effect).
Fat Oxidation: Enhanced (glucagon agonism).

Case Prototype — The Quint Stack
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 400, GH 4 IU/day, Humalog 6 IU peri-workout, Retatrutide 8 mg weekly,
Cagrilintide.
Baseline Labs: A1c 5.5, TG 160, HDL 36.
12 Weeks: A1c ↓ 5.2, TG ↓ 90, HDL ↑ 48, IGF-1 ↑ 410.
Result: 10 lbs lean gain, negligible fat gain, metabolic health improved despite
GH/insulin use.

9) The Takeaway
GLP-1 and GIP agonists are not magic fat burners — they are metabolic stabilizers. They let
you run GH, insulin, and high-calorie bulks while keeping bloodwork from collapsing.
The next era of bodybuilding enhancement isn’t just “more gear.” It’s smarter stacks that
balance anabolism with metabolic control.
•
•
•

Old-school formula: Test + GH + insulin = Growth (but with risk).
Modern formula: Test + GH + insulin + GLP-1/GIP ± Amylin = Growth + Control.
Future formula: Quint-agonist single molecules — but until then, bodybuilders can
build their own.

This isn’t about “getting shredded with less hunger.” It’s about making performance
enhancement sustainable — extending careers, preserving health, and keeping athletes in the
game long after reckless peers crash out.

\ Chapter 11 — Cardiovascular Risk &

Lipids
The stage lights only show the muscles. They don’t show the arteries, the endothelial walls, or
the slow hardening that can cut a career — or a life — short. If you’re running PEDs, your lipid
profile is almost certainly taking a beating. Some compounds wreck it faster, some slower, but
none leave it untouched.
The harsh truth: bodybuilders aren’t dying of biceps tears — they’re dying of heart attacks.
This chapter is about how to see it coming and what to do about it.

1) Steroid-Induced Dyslipidemia: The Silent Killer
•

•

•

DHT-Derivatives (Anavar, Winstrol, Masteron, Superdrol):
o Slash HDL (often <20 mg/dL).
o Raise LDL and ApoB.
o Accelerate arterial plaque formation.
19-Nors (Tren, Nandrolone, Ment):
o Mixed HDL/LDL impact.
o Raise blood pressure → compounding vascular strain.
o Worsen insulin resistance, indirectly harming lipids.
Testosterone (supraphysiologic doses):
o Modestly lowers HDL.
o Raises ApoB through LDL turnover.
o Less destructive than orals, but still contributes.

PEDs always shift lipids in the wrong direction. The only question is how fast, and how
severe.

2) The Bloodwork That Actually Matters
A basic cholesterol panel doesn’t cut it. Serious athletes need advanced lipid and
cardiovascular markers:
•
•
•
•
•

ApoB: The most predictive risk marker. Tracks LDL particle number.
LDL-P: Particle count, more predictive than LDL-C.
HDL-C: <25 mg/dL = red zone.
Triglycerides: GH + insulin elevate dramatically. >150 mg/dL = risk.
Lipoprotein(a): Genetic factor, high levels multiply PED risk.

•

hs-CRP: Inflammation marker; chronic elevation = endothelial stress.

3) Field Patterns in Enhanced Athletes
•

•

•

Oral-heavy cycles (Winstrol, Anavar, Superdrol):
o HDL crashes <15.
o LDL shoots >200.
o ApoB extreme elevations.
GH + insulin bulks:
o Triglycerides spike.
o A1c drifts upward → compounded vascular damage.
Moderate Test/Deca cycles:
o ApoB rises, HDL declines gradually.
o Chronic use still builds cumulative damage.

4) Interventions That Actually Work
Pharmaceutical Rescue (Traditional)
•
•
•

Statins (rosuvastatin, pitavastatin): Lower LDL, ApoB.
Ezetimibe: 15–20% LDL reduction, synergistic with statins.
PCSK9 inhibitors: Best LDL lowering available, but costly.

These remain gold standards for pure LDL/ApoB reduction. But they don’t fix HDL collapse,
triglyceride spikes, or insulin resistance from PEDs.

The New Frontier — Retatrutide (Reta)
Retatrutide is a GLP-1 + GIP + Glucagon triple agonist.
•
•
•
•
•

HbA1c ↓ ~2%.
Triglycerides ↓ 20–40%.
LDL ↓ modestly.
HDL ↑ modestly.
Fat oxidation ↑ (via glucagon agonism).

Why this matters:
•

DHTs wreck HDL and spike LDL.

•
•

GH + insulin raise triglycerides and A1c.
Retatrutide addresses all three: lipids, glucose, and fat oxidation.

It’s not a replacement for statins when ApoB is sky-high — but it’s a powerful adjunct that
specifically fixes the metabolic wreckage PEDs cause.

5) Practical Risk Mitigation
•
•
•

•

Pull labs baseline → mid-cycle → post-cycle.
If HDL <20, LDL >160, or ApoB >120 → act now.
Stack options:
o Severe LDL/ApoB: Statin + ezetimibe.
o GH/insulin-induced triglycerides + A1c rise: Retatrutide or Tirzepatide.
o Mild HDL drop: Fish oil + citrus bergamot (adjunct only).
For advanced setups: GH + Retatrutide ± Cagrilintide (amylin) = next-level metabolic
control.

6) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Oral King
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 300, Anavar 50, Winstrol 50.
Labs (6 weeks): HDL 12, LDL 210, ApoB 160.
Fix: Rosuvastatin 10 mg + ezetimibe 10 mg.
Outcome (8 weeks later): HDL 20, LDL 95, ApoB 80. Still poor, but catastrophic risk
reduced.

Case B — The GH/insulin Bulk
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 500, GH 6 IU/day, Humalog 10 IU/day.
Labs (12 weeks): TG 230, HDL 32, A1c 5.8.
Fix: Retatrutide 8 mg weekly.
Outcome (8 weeks later): TG ↓ 110, HDL ↑ 38, A1c 5.4. IGF-1 preserved.

Case C — The Quint Protocol
•

Stack: Test 400, GH 4 IU/day, Retatrutide 8 mg weekly, Cagrilintide.

•
•

Labs (12 weeks): A1c 5.2, TG 90, HDL 44, LDL 115, ApoB down.
Outcome: Cleanest lipid profile athlete ever maintained on GH/insulin stack.

8) The Takeaway
PEDs break lipids. Period. HDL tanks, LDL and ApoB rise, triglycerides spike, and glucose
drifts upward. This is the number one silent killer in bodybuilding.
But now there’s a new weapon:
•
•
•

Statins and ezetimibe remain essential for LDL/ApoB rescue.
Retatrutide is a game-changer for triglycerides, A1c, and modest HDL/LDL repair.
Combined with GH and cagrilintide, it builds a quint metabolic shield against the chaos
PEDs cause.

The lesson is simple:
•
•
•

If labs are bad → act.
If labs are borderline → monitor.
If you’re enhanced → plan for lipid control proactively.

Because the truth is brutal: no one cares how shredded you looked on stage if your career — and
your life — ends 20 years too soon.

Chapter 12 — Liver & Kidney Health
Your liver and kidneys don’t win shows. They don’t make you look fuller, leaner, or harder. But
they keep you alive to actually step on stage. In enhanced bodybuilding, these two organs are
under constant assault — and they don’t complain until it’s too late.

1) Liver Stress in Bodybuilding
Causes of Stress
•
•
•
•

Oral Anabolics (C17-aa): Anadrol, Dianabol, Superdrol, Halotestin. Direct
hepatotoxicity.
Overfeeding in Bulking: High-calorie diets → fatty liver (NAFLD).
Excessive NSAID use: Many athletes use ibuprofen/naproxen for joint pain → worsens
hepatotoxicity.
Alcohol (yes, some still drink): Adds unnecessary double stress.

Labs to Watch
•
•
•
•

ALT, AST: Enzyme “spikes” are common, but may reflect muscle breakdown. Context
is key.
Alkaline Phosphatase (ALP): More specific to bile duct stress.
GGT (Gamma-Glutamyl Transferase): Best marker for drug-induced liver injury.
Bilirubin: Elevated = cholestasis (bile flow issues).

Patterns in Athletes
•
•
•

Oral cycles: ALT/AST 2–5x normal, GGT elevated.
GH + insulin bulks: Fatty liver signatures, elevated ALT.
Contest prep (drying agents, orals): Bilirubin + ALP creep up.

2) Kidney Stress in Bodybuilding
Causes of Stress
•
•
•
•
•

High Protein Intake: Usually tolerated, but in pre-existing CKD can accelerate decline.
Dehydration/Diuretics: Contest prep → acute kidney injury risk.
NSAIDs: Crush renal blood flow.
High Blood Pressure: The silent kidney killer in PED users.
19-Nors (Tren, Deca): Evidence of direct renal stress.

Labs to Watch
•
•
•
•

Creatinine: Not reliable alone (muscle mass raises baseline).
eGFR: Better, but still skewed in muscular athletes.
Cystatin-C: Best available GFR marker for athletes.
Urinalysis (protein, microalbumin): Early red flag for kidney damage.

Patterns in Athletes
•
•
•

Tren/Nandrolone cycles: BP ↑, creatinine ↑, eGFR ↓.
Diuretic-heavy preps: Transient acute kidney stress, sometimes irreversible.
Chronic blast-and-cruise: Slow GFR decline if unchecked.

3) The “Field Rules” of Organ Monitoring
•
•
•
•

ALT/AST alone ≠ liver failure → check GGT to confirm.
Creatinine alone ≠ kidney failure → check Cystatin-C.
Persistent enzyme elevations = action needed.
Spikes after oral cycles = remove orals, re-test.

4) Interventions That Actually Work
Liver Protection
•
•
•
•
•

Stop orals when labs spike → no supplement “saves” you mid-cycle.
UDCA (Ursodiol): Best for cholestasis; pharma-level support.
TUDCA: OTC version, solid support against oral-induced bile stasis.
NAC (N-acetyl cysteine): Glutathione precursor, protective.
Avoid alcohol + NSAIDs in cycle.

Kidney Protection
•
•
•
•
•

Blood pressure control = king. ARBs (telmisartan) protect kidneys beyond lowering
BP.
Hydration: Minimum 4–5 liters/day enhanced; more if sweating heavily.
Avoid contest diuretic abuse: Acute renal shutdown kills more bodybuilders than we
admit.
GLP-1/GIP agonists (Tirz, Reta): Lower A1c and triglycerides → reduce kidney
vascular strain.
Reta (Retatrutide):
o ↓ HbA1c, ↓ TG → reduces “diabetic-like” kidney damage from GH + insulin use.

o

Improves fat oxidation → lowers NAFLD risk, indirectly supporting liver.

5) Case Vignettes
Case A — The Oral Crusher
•

•

Stack: Test 400, Superdrol 30 mg, Anadrol 50 mg.
Labs (6 weeks): ALT 120, AST 140, GGT 180.
Fix: Orals dropped, TUDCA + NAC, rehydration.
Follow-up: ALT/AST down 50%, GGT normalized.

Case B — The Tren Bulk
•
•
•
•

Stack: Test 250, Tren 400, GH 6 IU/day, insulin 8 IU/day.
Labs: Creatinine 1.9, eGFR ↓, A1c 6.0, TG 220.
Fix: Added Retatrutide 8 mg weekly, telmisartan 40 mg.
Follow-up (12 weeks): A1c ↓ 5.5, TG ↓ 120, BP 120/75, cystatin-C normalized.

Case C — The Prep Gone Wrong
•

•

Stack: Test 200, Tren 400, Winstrol 50, Lasix (diuretic).
Labs (peak week): Creatinine 2.1, eGFR 45, urinalysis protein+.
Fix: Pulled diuretics, aggressive rehydration, hospital monitoring.
Outcome: Partial recovery, but baseline kidney function permanently reduced.
